English translation

Based on the research conducted, I provide a detailed answer to your question in Lithuanian:

Is the US a FIMI Threat to Europe?

Short answer: In official EU documents, the United States is not designated as a FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) threat actor in the same context as Russia or China. However, since 2024–2025, concerns have been rapidly growing regarding US interference in European democratic processes, particularly through social media platforms and political support for the far-right.

1. Official EU Position: The US – Partner, Not Enemy

In the European External Action Service (EEAS) FIMI threat reports (2023–2026), the primary FIMI actors are consistently identified as:

  • Russia (approx. 29% of attributed incidents)
  • China (approx. 6% of attributed incidents)

The US is described here as an ally and partner in the fight against FIMI. The EU and the US have established a joint methodology for FIMI identification (DISARM framework, STIX 2 standard, OpenCTI platform) through the Trade and Technology Council (TTC).

> "The European Union and the United States have established a close partnership in addressing the issue of information manipulation..." – EEAS documents

2. Recent Developments (2024–2025): Growing Concern

Despite the official partnership, serious tensions have emerged since late 2024:

a) US National Security Strategy (December 2025) The strategy document states that the US will "cultivate resistance" in Europe in order to "correct its current trajectory," and praises the growing influence of "patriotic parties." This provoked a sharp reaction:

> "Allies do not threaten to interfere in the domestic political choices of allies... We cannot accept the threat of interference in European political life." – António Costa, President of the European Council (December 8, 2025)

b) US Officials' Interference in Elections - JD Vance (US Vice President) met with the leader of the German far-right AfD, Alice Weidel, in Munich in February 2025, ahead of the elections. Berlin assessed this as "unacceptable electoral interference." - Elon Musk (owner of X/Twitter, Trump administration ally) publicly supported the AfD in Germany, criticized the annulment of the Romanian elections, and called judges "tyrants."

c) Influence of Social Media Platforms EU regulators are investigating whether the X algorithm was manipulated to increase the reach of politicians supported by Musk. The European Commission is conducting investigations against X, Meta, and TikTok under the Digital Services Act (DSA) regarding potential electoral manipulation.

d) US Criticism of EU Regulation The Trump administration has criticized the EU Digital Services Act (DSA) as "censorship" and ordered diplomats to run lobbying campaigns against the law. This creates tension regarding the regulation of the information space.

3. Essential Difference: FIMI vs. Political Influence

It is important to distinguish between two categories:

FIMI (as defined by the EU) US Activity in Europe
Covert, coordinated disinformation campaigns Public political statements
Bot networks, fake news pages Support for specific parties (AfD, Reform UK, National Front)
Designated as "Russia" and "China" Influence of social media platform owners
Sanctions, diplomatic measures Diplomatic pressure, trade threats

The US is not officially included in the EU FIMI sanctions lists like Russia or China. However, in December 2025, the situation is changing – the EU is increasingly discussing "hybrid threats" from various sources, including allies.

4. EU Response: European Democracy Shield

In November 2025, the European Commission introduced the European Democracy Shield, which includes:

  • Protection of the integrity of the information space
  • Protection of the electoral process from foreign interference
  • Strengthening of societal resilience

Official documents of this shield still focus primarily on Russia, but it is mentioned that "various actors" may have an interest in undermining EU democracy.

Conclusion

At the official level, the US is not considered a FIMI threat to Europe – they are key partners in the fight against Russian and Chinese disinformation.

However, in practice, a new reality has been taking shape since 2024–2025: the US administration and related actors (Elon Musk, JD Vance) are actively interfering in European politics, supporting far-right parties, and criticizing EU regulation. This raises serious questions regarding transatlantic relations and the protection of European democratic processes.

European leaders have already publicly warned Washington against interference, but for now, there is no official classification of the US into the FIMI threat category – this remains a diplomatic "grey area" between allied cooperation and unacceptable interference in internal affairs.

Original text

Remdamasis atliktais tyrimais, pateikiu išsamų atsakymą į jūsų klausimą lietuvių kalba:

Ar JAV yra FIMI grėsmė Europai?

Trumpas atsakymas: Oficialiose ES dokumentuose Jungtinės Valstijos nėra įvardijamos kaip FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) grėsmės veikėjos tame pačiame kontekste kaip Rusija ar Kinija. Tačiau nuo 2024–2025 m. sparčiai auga susirūpinimas dėl JAV įsikišimo į Europos demokratinius procesus, ypač per socialinės žiniasklaidos platformas ir politinę paramą kraštutiniams dešiniesiems.

1. Oficiali ES pozicija: JAV – partneris, ne priešas

Europos išorės veiksmų tarnybos (EEAS) FIMI grėsmių ataskaitose (2023–2026 m.) nuosekliai kaip pagrindiniai FIMI veikėjai įvardijami:

  • Rusija (apie 29 % priskirtų incidentų)
  • Kinija (apie 6 % priskirtų incidentų)

JAV čia aprašomos kaip sąjungininkė ir partnerė kovoje su FIMI. ES ir JAV yra sukūrusios bendrą metodologiją FIMI identifikavimui (DISARM sistema, STIX 2 standartas, OpenCTI platforma) per Prekybos ir technologijų tarybą (TTC).

> „Europos Sąjunga ir Jungtinės Valstijos yra sukūrusios glaudžią partnerystę sprendžiant manipuliavimo informacija klausimus..." – EEAS dokumentai

2. Naujausi pokyčiai (2024–2025 m.): augantis susirūpinimas

Nepaisant oficialios partnerystės, nuo 2024 m. pabaigos išryškėjo rimtų įtampų:

a) JAV Nacionalinio saugumo strategija (2025 m. gruodis) Strategijos dokumente teigiama, kad JAV „kultivuos pasipriešinimą" Europoje, siekdama „pataisyti jos dabartinę kryptį", ir giria „patriotinių partijų" augančią įtaką. Tai sukėlė aštrią reakciją:

> „Sąjungininkai negraso kištis į sąjungininkų vidaus politinius pasirinkimus... Mes negalime priimti grėsmės kištis į Europos politinį gyvenimą." – António Costa, Europos Vadovų Tarybos pirmininkas (2025 m. gruodžio 8 d.)

b) JAV pareigūnų įsikišimas į rinkimus - JD Vance (JAV viceprezidentas) 2025 m. vasarį Miunchene susitiko su Vokietijos kraštutinių dešiniųjų AfD lyderė Alice Weidel, prieš rinkimus. Berlynas tai įvertino kaip „nepriimtiną kišimąsi į rinkimus". - Elon Musk (X/Twitter savininkas, Trump administracijos sąjungininkas) viešai palaikė AfD Vokietijoje, kritikavo Rumunijos rinkimų panaikinimą ir vadino teisėjus „tironais".

c) Socialinės žiniasklaidos platformų įtaka ES reguliuotojai tiria, ar X algoritmas nebuvo manipuliuojamas siekiant padidinti Musk palaikomų politikų pasiekiamumą. Europos Komisija atlieka tyrimus prieš X, Meta ir TikTok pagal Skaitmeninių paslaugų aktą (DSA) dėl galimo manipuliavimo rinkimais.

d) JAV kritika ES reguliavimui Trumpo administracija kritikuoja ES Skaitmeninių paslaugų aktą (DSA) kaip „censūrą" ir įsakė diplomatams lobistines kampanijas prieš šį įstatymą. Tai sukuria įtampą dėl informacinės erdvės reguliavimo.

3. Esminis skirtumas: FIMI vs. politinė įtaka

Svarbu atskirti dvi kategorijas:

FIMI (kaip apibrėžta ES) JAV veikla Europoje
Slaptos, koordinuotos dezinformacijos kampanijos Vieši politiniai pareiškimai
Botų tinklai, netikri naujienų puslapiai Parama konkrečioms partijoms (AfD, Reform UK, Nacionalinis frontas)
Įvardijama kaip „Rusija" ir „Kinija" Socialinės žiniasklaidos platformų savininkų įtaka
Sankcijos, diplomatinės priemonės Diplomatinis spaudimas, prekybos grasinimai

JAV nėra oficialiai įtrauktos į ES FIMI sankcijų sąrašus kaip Rusija ar Kinija. Tačiau 2025 m. gruodžio mėn. situacija keičiasi – ES vis aktyviau diskutuoja apie „hibridines grėsmes" iš įvairių šaltinių, įskaitant sąjungininkes.

4. ES atsakas: Europos demokratijos skydas

2025 m. lapkritį Europos Komisija pristatė Europos demokratijos skydą (European Democracy Shield), kuris apima:

  • Informacinės erdvės vientisumo apsaugą
  • Rinkimų proceso apsaugą nuo užsienio kišimosi
  • Visuomenės atsparumo stiprinimą

Oficialiose šio skydo dokumentuose vis dar daugiausia dėmesio skiriama Rusijai, tačiau paminėta, kad „įvairūs veikėjai" gali turėti interesą pakenkti ES demokratijai.

Išvada

Oficialiame lygmenyje JAV nėra laikomos FIMI grėsme Europai – jos yra svarbiausios partnerės kovoje su Rusijos ir Kinijos dezinformacija.

Tačiau praktikoje nuo 2024–2025 m. formuojasi nauja realybė: JAV administracija ir su ja susiję veikėjai (Elon Musk, JD Vance) aktyviai kišasi į Europos politiką, palaiko kraštutinių dešiniųjų partijas ir kritikuoja ES reguliavimą. Tai kelia rimtų klausimų dėl transatlantinių santykių ir Europos demokratinių procesų apsaugos.

Europos lyderiai jau viešai perspėja Vašingtoną dėl kišimosi, tačiau kol kas nėra oficialaus JAV priskyrimo FIMI grėsmių kategorijai – tai išlieka diplomatine „pilkąja zona" tarp sąjungininkų bendradarbiavimo ir nepriimtino kišimosi į vidaus reikalus.

English translation

Based on the analysis of official EU documents, EEAS (European External Action Service) reports, and current news from 2024–2026, the following answer can be provided:

Short Answer

Officially, the US is not classified by the European Union as a FIMI (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) threat in the same sense as Russia or China. However, in 2025 the Trump administration's actions caused unprecedented concern in Europe and were characterized by a number of European leaders as unacceptable interference in internal affairs, creating a new dynamic in transatlantic relations.

1. Official EU Position: The US is a Partner, Not a Threat

In official EEAS reports on FIMI: - The 3rd EEAS Report (March 2025) and the 4th Report (2026) explicitly state that the main sources of FIMI for Europe are Russia (29% of documented incidents) and China (6%). - The US is mentioned exclusively as a key partner in countering FIMI: the EU and the US have developed a joint threat analysis methodology (the DISARM framework), cooperated within the Trade and Technology Council (TTC), and exchanged intelligence.

Prior to 2025, transatlantic cooperation in countering disinformation was considered "exemplary."

2. Sharp Shift in the Situation in 2025

The Trump administration took a number of steps that European leaders perceived as a threat to democratic processes:

a) Dismantling US Anti-Disinformation Structures The US closed key offices dealing with counter-foreign interference: - The Counter Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference Hub (State Department) - The Global Engagement Centre - The Foreign Malign Influence Center (Intelligence Community)

This happened against the backdrop of Russia continuing to step up its FIMI efforts in Europe.

b) Direct Interference in European Elections - Romania (2025): Vice President JD Vance at the Munich Security Conference (February 2025) criticized the cancellation of the Romanian presidential election, stating that "closing off disfavored views is the surest way to destroy democracy." The US openly demanded that the right-wing candidate Călin Georgescu, supported by Trump, be allowed to run in the election. - Germany (February 2025): Vance met with the leader of the AfD party (classified as extremist) on the eve of the parliamentary elections. Chancellor Olaf Scholz called this "direct interference" in democracy: "We do not accept that outsiders interfere in our democracy, in our elections." - Poland (2025): Trump personally endorsed right-wing candidate Karol Nawrocki in the presidential election.

c) New US National Security Strategy (December 2025) A document published by the White House contains unprecedented criticism of Europe: - Accuses the EU of "undermining democratic processes," "censoring freedom of speech," and "suppressing political opposition." - States that Europe risks "civilizational erasure" due to migration. - Calls on the US to "cultivate resistance" within Europe to "correct its current course." - Openly supports Europe's nationalist right-wing parties.

Reaction: European Council President António Costa stated: "Allies do not threaten interference in the internal political elections of their allies. We cannot accept the threat of interference in European political life."

3. Important Distinction: FIMI vs. Political Pressure from an Ally

It is necessary to distinguish between two concepts:

FIMI (as a threat) US Actions (2025)
Covert operations of hostile states (Russia, China) Open political pressure from an ally
Disinformation, bots, fake media Public support for parties, diplomatic pressure
Goal: undermining democracy Goal: changing Europe's political course
Viewed in EEAS reports as a threat Not classified as FIMI, but causes a crisis of trust

Experts (for example, from the EUISS — European Union Institute for Security Studies) note that the US is rolling back cooperation on countering disinformation, which weakens the overall transatlantic defense against Russia and China, but does not make the US a source of FIMI in the classical sense.

4. Technological Aspect: Pressure on US Platforms

The EU applies the Digital Services Act (DSA) to American tech giants (X, Meta), fining them for inadequate efforts against disinformation. The Trump administration perceives this as "censorship" and "extortion" of American companies, threatening tariffs and restrictions.

This creates tension, but is not qualified as FIMI on the part of the US.

Conclusions

  1. Formally, the US is not included on the list of FIMI threats to Europe — the main threats stem from Russia and China.

  2. Factically, an unprecedented crisis occurred in transatlantic relations in 2025: the Trump administration's actions (support for Eurosceptics, election interference, ideological pressure) are perceived by European leaders as unacceptable foreign interference, even if it comes from an ally.

  3. Paradox: The US, which traditionally has been Europe's main partner in countering FIMI, is now weakening this fight itself, shutting down its own structures and criticizing European approaches to disinformation regulation.

  4. New Reality: Europe is forced to strengthen its own institutions (European Democracy Shield, FIMI-combating center) without US support, which changes the balance of power in the transatlantic partnership.

Thus, the US is not a FIMI threat in the classical sense (like Russia), but its actions in 2025 create a new type of threat — political and ideological pressure from an ally, which European leaders consider unacceptable interference in sovereign democratic processes.

Original text

На основе анализа официальных документов ЕС, отчетов EEAS (Европейской службы внешних связей) и актуальных новостей 2024–2026 годов, можно дать следующий ответ:

Краткий ответ

Официально США не классифицируются Европейским союзом как угроза FIMI (иностранного манипулирования информацией и вмешательства) в том же смысле, что Россия или Китай. Однако в 2025 году действия администрации Трампа вызвали беспрецедентную озабоченность в Европе и были охарактеризованы рядом европейских лидеров как недопустимое вмешательство во внутренние дела, что создает новую динамику в трансатлантических отношениях.

1. Официальная позиция ЕС: США — партнер, а не угроза

В официальных отчетах EEAS о FIMI: - 3-й отчет EEAS (март 2025) и 4-й отчет (2026) прямо указывают, что основными источниками FIMI для Европы являются Россия (29% задокументированных инцидентов) и Китай (6%) . - США упоминаются исключительно как ключевой партнер в борьбе с FIMI: ЕС и США разработали общую методологию анализа угроз (DISARM framework), сотрудничали в рамках Совета по торговле и технологиям (TTC) и обменивались разведданными .

До 2025 года трансатлантическое сотрудничество в сфере противодействия дезинформации считалось «образцовым».

2. Резкое изменение ситуации в 2025 году

Администрация Трампа предприняла ряд шагов, которые европейские лидеры восприняли как угрозу демократическим процессам:

а) Демонтаж американских структур по борьбе с дезинформацией США закрыли ключевые офисы, занимавшиеся противодействием иностранному вмешательству: - Counter Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference Hub (Госдепартамент) - Global Engagement Centre - Foreign Malign Influence Center (разведывательное сообщество)

Это произошло на фоне того, что Россия продолжает наращивать усилия по FIMI в Европе .

б) Прямое вмешательство в европейские выборы - Румыния (2025): Вице-президент JD Vance на Мюнхенской конференции безопасности (февраль 2025) раскритиковал отмену президентских выборов в Румынии, заявив, что «закрытие неугодных взглядов — самый верный способ разрушить демократию». США открыто потребовали допустить к выборам правого кандидата Калина Джорджеску, которого поддерживает Трамп . - Германия (февраль 2025): Vance встретился с лидером партии AfD (классифицированной как экстремистская) накануне парламентских выборов. Канцлер Олаф Шольц назвал это «прямым вмешательством» в демократию: «Мы не принимаем, чтобы посторонние вмешивались в нашу демократию, в наши выборы». - Польша (2025): Трамп лично поддержал правого кандидата Кароля Навроцкого на президентских выборах.

в) Новая стратегия национальной безопасности США (декабрь 2025) Документ, опубликованный Белым домом, содержит беспрецедентную критику Европы: - Обвиняет ЕС в «подрыве демократических процессов», «цензуре свободы слова» и «подавлении политической оппозиции» - Заявляет, что Европа рискует «цивилизационным исчезновением» из-за миграции - Призывает США «культивировать сопротивление» внутри Европы для «коррекции её текущего курса» - Открыто поддерживает националистические правые партии Европы .

Реакция: Президент Европейского совета Антониу Кошта заявил: «Союзники не угрожают вмешательством во внутренние политические выборы своих союзников. Мы не можем принять угрозу вмешательства в европейскую политическую жизнь» .

3. Важное различие: FIMI vs. политическое давление союзника

Необходимо различать два понятия:

FIMI (как угроза) Действия США (2025)
Тайные операции враждебных государств (Россия, Китай) Открытое политическое давление союзника
Дезинформация, боты, фейковые СМИ Публичная поддержка партий, дипломатическое давление
Цель: подрыв демократии Цель: изменение политического курса Европы
Рассматривается в отчетах EEAS как угроза Не классифицируется как FIMI, но вызывает кризис доверия

Эксперты (например, из EUISS — Института ЕС по исследованиям в области безопасности) отмечают, что США сворачивают сотрудничество по борьбе с дезинформацией, что ослабляет общую трансатлантическую оборону против России и Китая, но не делают США источником FIMI в классическом понимании .

4. Технологический аспект: давление на американские платформы

ЕС применяет Закон о цифровых услугах (DSA) к американским технологическим гигантам (X, Meta), штрафуя их за недостаточную борьбу с дезинформацией. Администрация Трампа воспринимает это как «цензуру» и «вымогательство» американских компаний, угрожая тарифами и ограничениями .

Это создает напряженность, но не квалифицируется как FIMI со стороны США.

Выводы

  1. Формально США не включены в список угроз FIMI для Европы — основные угрозы исходят от России и Китая.

  2. Фактически в 2025 году произошел беспрецедентный кризис в трансатлантических отношениях: действия администрации Трампа (поддержка евроскептиков, вмешательство в выборы, идеологическое давление) воспринимаются европейскими лидерами как недопустимое иностранное вмешательство, даже если оно исходит от союзника.

  3. Парадокс: США, которые традиционно были главным партнером Европы в борьбе с FIMI, теперь сами ослабляют эту борьбу, закрывая собственные структуры и критикуя европейские подходы к регулированию дезинформации.

  4. Новая реальность: Европа вынуждена укреплять собственные институты (Европейский щит демократии, центр по борьбе с FIMI) без поддержки США, что меняет баланс сил в трансатлантическом партнерстве.

Таким образом, США не являются угрозой FIMI в классическом понимании (как Россия), но их действия в 2025 году создают новый тип угрозы — политическое и идеологическое давление со стороны союзника, которое европейские лидеры считают неприемлемым вмешательством в суверенные демократические процессы.

English — answer round 1

Web access: Used — native search

Sources returned by search: - Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS - https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf - https://www.osce.org/files/f/documents/d/b/593720_0.pdf - EU drops sponsorship of disinformation conference at Trump officials’ request | Trump administration | The Guardian - EU official warns US against European interference | AP News

English translation

Yes—as a credible risk Europe should assess, but not as a blanket label for the United States or every American attempt to influence European politics. The crucial distinction is between foreign influence, political interference, and the narrower category of foreign information manipulation and interference. The EU’s definition makes manipulative conduct—not an actor’s nationality or alliance status—the central test. (eeas.europa.eu)

What would make American activity FIMI?

The European External Action Service (EEAS) defines FIMI as intentional, coordinated, manipulative behaviour that threatens or could negatively affect political processes, procedures, or values. It can involve state or non-state actors, and need not be illegal. Consequently, neither being a NATO ally nor being a private American company automatically puts an actor outside the definition. (eeas.europa.eu)

But criticism of European governments, support for a political party, or disagreement over speech regulation is not automatically FIMI. Applying that definition requires evidence of manipulation and coordination, rather than simply an unwelcome opinion. Examples worth investigating would include concealed sponsorship, deceptive identities, coordinated inauthentic amplification, or deliberate manipulation of information distribution. That is a conduct-based assessment, not a test of political ideology. (eeas.europa.eu)

Why the concern is credible

There are concrete reasons to scrutinise U.S. actors:

  • An explicit policy of influencing Europe’s political direction. The U.S. National Security Strategy, released in December 2025, calls for “cultivating resistance to Europe’s current trajectory within European nations.” This establishes an intention to encourage political change inside European countries. It does not, by itself, establish that the means used are manipulative or constitute FIMI. (whitehouse.gov)
  • A platform owner’s intervention in an election. In its report on Germany’s February 23, 2025 election, OSCE/ODIHR documented Elon Musk’s vigorous support for the AfD through X, giving it unprecedented prominence. It also recorded the European Commission’s investigation into X’s recommender system. This warrants scrutiny, but the report does not establish that Musk secretly manipulated the algorithm or acted under U.S. government direction. (osce.org)
  • Pressure on the counter-disinformation community. On October 7, 2026, the Guardian reported U.S. pressure to withdraw sponsorship of a European disinformation conference—pressure, but not itself proven FIMI. (theguardian.com)

European Council President António Costa also publicly warned against U.S. interference in European politics on December 8, 2025. That demonstrates an official concern about interference, although it is not equivalent to a technical attribution of a FIMI operation. (apnews.com)

Is this the EU’s official classification?

Not in the same sense as its established treatment of Russian and Chinese operations. The EEAS publicly identifies Russia and China as its particular focus. That focus should not be mistaken for a definition that exempts other countries, but neither should an analytical concern about U.S. actors be presented as an established EU-wide designation. (eeas.europa.eu)

My assessment: Europe should treat American state-linked and private actors as possible FIMI perpetrators and investigate specific conduct using the same evidentiary standards applied to anyone else. The evidence supports a credible interference risk; it does not justify calling all American influence FIMI or assuming every private American intervention is a U.S. government operation. An ally should receive neither automatic immunity nor automatic guilt. (eeas.europa.eu)

French — answer round 1

Web access: Used — native search

Sources returned by search: - https://www.iss.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2025-10/CP_187_ch4.pdf - INTRODUCTION - The 2025 US National Security Strategy - Trump admin bars 5 Europeans it says led drive to "censor" U.S. views online - Musk hosts German far-right leader on X, stirs angst about election meddling By Reuters - EU drops sponsorship of disinformation conference at Trump officials’ request | Trump administration | The Guardian - Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS

English translation

Yes, certain American actors can represent a FIMI threat to Europe. But broadly qualifying "the United States" as a FIMI actor would be too imprecise: a distinction must be made between the government, companies, individuals, and effectively documented operations. The available evidence justifies vigilance toward the Trump administration and certain private American actors, without automatically equating their actions to Russian or Chinese operations. (iss.europa.eu)

1. What falls — or does not fall — under FIMI

According to the European External Action Service (EEAS), FIMI refers to intentional, coordinated, and manipulative behaviors that are likely to undermine values, procedures, and political processes. They can be carried out by state or non-state actors, and are not necessarily illegal. This definition grants no exemption to allied countries. (eeas.europa.eu)

Conversely, American criticism of the EU, public political support, or diplomatic pressure are not enough, on their own, to establish a FIMI operation. The methods must be examined: deception, coordination, concealment, manipulation of dissemination, and harm to democratic debate. The central distinction is therefore between influence, interference, and information manipulation. (eeas.europa.eu)

2. Why the American risk deserves to be taken seriously

An explicit desire to influence the European political trajectory

The U.S. national security strategy published on December 5, 2025 plans to "cultivate resistance to Europe's current trajectory within European nations" — a translation of the official text. This is an explicit signal of an intent to exert internal political influence. However, this does not in itself prove a clandestine or manipulative informational operation. (europarl.europa.eu)

Campaigns against European mechanisms to combat disinformation

An analysis by the EU Institute for Security Studies documents a coordinated campaign by several U.S. diplomatic representations in the spring of 2025, presenting Europe's fight against disinformation as an enterprise of censorship. It also highlights American interventions in favor of European parties and candidates. This constitutes a concerning institutional analysis, not a formal attribution of FIMI by the EEAS. (iss.europa.eu)

On December 23, 2025, Washington also imposed entry restrictions on five European figures, including Thierry Breton and officials from organizations working against disinformation or online hatred. Washington invoked the protection of American freedom of speech; France denounced this as pressure on European digital sovereignty. These measures constitute concrete pressure, but are not automatically a FIMI operation. (axios.com)

Private actors capable of massively influencing the debate

On January 9, 2025, Elon Musk used X to call on Germans to vote for the AfD. He also relayed the misleading claim that the EU could cancel an electoral victory by this party. This documents a political intervention and an amplification of misleading content; it is not enough to demonstrate a coordinated operation directed by the U.S. State, nor an effectively established algorithmic manipulation. (investing.com)

3. What is the conclusion for Europe?

My assessment is therefore: yes to identifying an American risk; no to a general accusation without precise attribution. Each operation must be evaluated using the same criteria, regardless of the nationality of its perpetrator.

The distinction is also political: on October 7, 2026, The Guardian reported that European officials were challenging the qualification of the United States as a disinformation actor. (theguardian.com)

The right approach would be to protect the European debate against manipulative behaviors, while preserving legitimate criticisms and political opinions. In other words: allied status must not create immunity, but American origin must not become proof of FIMI. This approach aligns with the behavioral logic of the EEAS framework. (eeas.europa.eu)

Original text

Oui, certains acteurs américains peuvent représenter une menace FIMI pour l’Europe. Mais qualifier globalement « les États-Unis » d’acteur FIMI serait trop imprécis : il faut distinguer le gouvernement, les entreprises, les individus et les opérations effectivement documentées. Les éléments disponibles justifient une vigilance envers l’administration Trump et certains acteurs privés américains, sans assimiler automatiquement leurs actions aux opérations russes ou chinoises. (iss.europa.eu)

1. Ce qui relève — ou non — de la FIMI

Selon le Service européen pour l’action extérieure (SEAE), la FIMI désigne des comportements intentionnels, coordonnés et manipulateurs, susceptibles de porter atteinte aux valeurs, aux procédures et aux processus politiques. Ils peuvent être menés par des acteurs étatiques ou non étatiques, et ne sont pas nécessairement illégaux. Cette définition n’accorde aucune exemption aux pays alliés. (eeas.europa.eu)

En revanche, une critique américaine de l’UE, un soutien politique public ou une pression diplomatique ne suffisent pas, à eux seuls, à établir une opération FIMI. Il faut examiner les méthodes : tromperie, coordination, dissimulation, manipulation de la diffusion et atteinte au débat démocratique. La distinction centrale est donc entre influence, ingérence et manipulation informationnelle. (eeas.europa.eu)

2. Pourquoi le risque américain mérite d’être pris au sérieux

Une volonté explicite d’influencer la trajectoire politique européenne

La stratégie américaine de sécurité nationale publiée le 5 décembre 2025 prévoit de « cultiver la résistance à la trajectoire actuelle de l’Europe au sein des nations européennes » — traduction du texte officiel. C’est un signal explicite de volonté d’influence politique interne. Cela ne prouve toutefois pas, en soi, une opération informationnelle clandestine ou manipulatrice. (europarl.europa.eu)

Des campagnes contre les dispositifs européens de lutte contre la désinformation

Une analyse de l’Institut d’études de sécurité de l’UE documente une campagne coordonnée de plusieurs représentations diplomatiques américaines, au printemps 2025, présentant la lutte européenne contre la désinformation comme une entreprise de censure. Elle souligne aussi les interventions américaines en faveur de partis et candidats européens. Il s’agit d’une analyse institutionnelle préoccupante, pas d’une attribution formelle de FIMI par le SEAE. (iss.europa.eu)

Le 23 décembre 2025, Washington a également imposé des restrictions d’entrée à cinq personnalités européennes, dont Thierry Breton et des responsables d’organisations travaillant contre la désinformation ou la haine en ligne. Washington invoquait la protection de la liberté d’expression américaine ; la France a dénoncé une pression sur la souveraineté numérique européenne. Ces mesures constituent une pression concrète, mais ne sont pas automatiquement une opération FIMI. (axios.com)

Des acteurs privés capables d’influencer massivement le débat

Le 9 janvier 2025, Elon Musk a utilisé X pour appeler les Allemands à voter pour l’AfD. Il a aussi relayé l’affirmation trompeuse selon laquelle l’UE pourrait annuler une victoire électorale de ce parti. Cela documente une intervention politique et une amplification de contenus trompeurs ; cela ne suffit pas à démontrer une opération coordonnée dirigée par l’État américain, ni une manipulation algorithmique effectivement établie. (investing.com)

3. Quelle conclusion pour l’Europe ?

Mon appréciation est donc : oui à l’identification d’un risque américain ; non à une accusation générale sans attribution précise. Il faut évaluer chaque opération selon les mêmes critères, quelle que soit la nationalité de son auteur.

La distinction est aussi politique : le 7 octobre 2026, le Guardian rapportait que des responsables européens contestaient la qualification des États-Unis comme acteur de désinformation. (theguardian.com)

La bonne approche serait de protéger le débat européen contre les comportements manipulateurs, tout en préservant les critiques légitimes et les opinions politiques. Autrement dit : le statut d’allié ne doit pas créer d’immunité, mais la provenance américaine ne doit pas devenir une preuve de FIMI. Cette approche correspond à la logique comportementale du cadre du SEAE. (eeas.europa.eu)

Lithuanian — answer round 1

Web access: Used — native search

Sources returned by search: - Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS - Musk uses X livestream to amplify German far-right leader's views ahead of an election | AP News - Vance meets German far-right AfD leader amid election interference criticism By Reuters - The 2025 US National Security Strategy | Think Tank | European Parliament

English translation

Yes, actors of U.S. origin can pose a FIMI threat to Europe, but it would be inaccurate to automatically label the entire U.S. state or any of its political influence as FIMI. In my view, public facts substantiate concern regarding interference, but specific information manipulation operations must be proven on a case-by-case basis. (eeas.europa.eu)

What is considered FIMI?

The European External Action Service (EEAS) defines FIMI as intentional, coordinated, manipulative behavior that threatens values, procedures, and political processes. It can be carried out by both state and non-state actors. Such activity is not necessarily illegal, and the focus of the assessment is not only on false content, but also on the techniques of manipulation. The definition is not limited to Russia or China. (eeas.europa.eu)

Therefore, applying this definition, the same criteria should apply to U.S. actors: it matters what they do and how they do it, rather than just what country they are from. However, public criticism, diplomatic disagreement, or openly expressed support for a party do not in themselves prove FIMI. (eeas.europa.eu)

Why is concern regarding U.S. actors substantiated?

  • Elon Musk’s involvement in the German elections. On January 9, 2025, he conversed on the "X" platform with Alice Weidel, leader of the "Alternative for Germany" (AfD) party, and urged people to vote for this party. This is a documented attempt to influence foreign voters' choices. Nevertheless, this conversation alone does not prove covert coordination or algorithmic manipulation; the European Commission also acknowledged his right to express an opinion. (apnews.com)
  • Political engagement by the U.S. administration. On February 14, 2025, Vice President J. D. Vance met with Weidel and criticized other parties for distancing themselves from the AfD. German officials viewed this as unwanted interference in elections. This case substantiates concern regarding political interference, but in itself is not yet proof of a coordinated FIMI operation. (investing.com)
  • Officially declared policy direction. The U.S. National Security Strategy published on December 5, 2025, outlines the promotion of resistance to the current European direction within European states. The European Parliamentary Research Service links this to an orientation toward ideologically aligned parties. In my view, this is a significant signal of interference risk, although strategic intent does not yet prove a specific manipulation operation. (europarl.europa.eu)

What conclusion would be most accurate?

It is reasonable for Europe to assess both U.S. state and private actors as potential sources of FIMI threats — without applying an exemption to allies. However, a distinction must be made between the actions of the U.S. administration, the activities of private individuals, and platform decisions: American origin alone does not prove direction by the U.S. government. Evidence of intent, coordination, and manipulative behavior is essential for a specific attribution of FIMI. (eeas.europa.eu)

At the same time, it should not be claimed that the scale of threats or the evidentiary base are already identical: the EEAS public threat assessment continues to pay special attention to Russia and China, while the March 2026 report documents systematic Russian operations against Europe. This does not negate the risk of U.S. origin, but it prevents all actors from being automatically equated. (eeas.europa.eu)

Original text

Taip, JAV kilmės veikėjai gali kelti FIMI grėsmę Europai, tačiau būtų netikslu visą JAV valstybę ar bet kokią jos politinę įtaką automatiškai vadinti FIMI. Mano vertinimu, vieši faktai pagrindžia susirūpinimą dėl kišimosi, bet konkrečias informacinio manipuliavimo operacijas reikia įrodyti atskirai. (eeas.europa.eu)

Kas laikoma FIMI?

Europos išorės veiksmų tarnyba (EIVT) FIMI apibrėžia kaip tyčinį, koordinuotą, manipuliacinį elgesį, kuris kelia grėsmę vertybėms, procedūroms ir politiniams procesams. Jį gali vykdyti tiek valstybės, tiek nevalstybiniai veikėjai. Tokia veikla nebūtinai yra neteisėta, o vertinimo centre yra ne vien melagingas turinys, bet ir manipuliavimo būdai. Apibrėžimas nėra apribotas Rusija ar Kinija. (eeas.europa.eu)

Todėl, taikant šį apibrėžimą, JAV veikėjams turėtų galioti tie patys kriterijai: svarbu, ką ir kaip jie daro, o ne vien tai, iš kokios šalies yra. Tačiau vieša kritika, diplomatinis nesutarimas ar atvirai išreikšta parama partijai savaime dar neįrodo FIMI. (eeas.europa.eu)

Kodėl susirūpinimas JAV veikėjais pagrįstas?

  • Elono Musko įsitraukimas į Vokietijos rinkimus. 2025 m. sausio 9 d. jis platformoje „X“ kalbėjosi su „Alternatyvos Vokietijai“ (AfD) lydere Alice Weidel ir ragino balsuoti už šią partiją. Tai dokumentuotas bandymas paveikti užsienio rinkėjų pasirinkimą. Vis dėlto vien šis pokalbis neįrodo slapto koordinavimo ar manipuliavimo algoritmais; Europos Komisija taip pat pripažino jo teisę reikšti nuomonę. (apnews.com)
  • JAV administracijos politinis įsitraukimas. 2025 m. vasario 14 d. viceprezidentas J. D. Vance’as susitiko su Weidel ir kritikavo kitų partijų atsiribojimą nuo AfD. Vokietijos pareigūnai tai vertino kaip nepageidaujamą kišimąsi į rinkimus. Šis atvejis pagrindžia susirūpinimą politiniu kišimusi, tačiau savaime dar nėra įrodymas apie koordinuotą FIMI operaciją. (investing.com)
  • Oficialiai deklaruota politikos kryptis. 2025 m. gruodžio 5 d. paskelbtoje JAV nacionalinio saugumo strategijoje numatytas pasipriešinimo dabartinei Europos krypčiai skatinimas Europos valstybėse. Europos Parlamento tyrimų tarnyba tai sieja su orientacija į ideologiškai artimas partijas. Mano vertinimu, tai reikšmingas kišimosi rizikos signalas, nors strateginis ketinimas dar neįrodo konkrečios manipuliavimo operacijos. (europarl.europa.eu)

Kokia išvada būtų tiksliausia?

Europai pagrįsta vertinti ir JAV valstybinius bei privačius veikėjus kaip galimus FIMI grėsmės šaltinius — netaikant sąjungininkams išimties. Tačiau reikia atskirti JAV administracijos veiksmus, privačių asmenų veiklą ir platformų sprendimus: vien amerikietiška kilmė neįrodo JAV valdžios vadovavimo. Konkrečiam FIMI priskyrimui būtini tyčios, koordinavimo ir manipuliacinio elgesio įrodymai. (eeas.europa.eu)

Kartu nereikėtų teigti, kad grėsmių mastas ar įrodymų bazė jau yra vienodi: EIVT viešame grėsmių vertinime ypatingas dėmesys tebėra skiriamas Rusijai ir Kinijai, o 2026 m. kovo ataskaita dokumentuoja sistemines Rusijos operacijas prieš Europą. Tai nepaneigia JAV kilmės rizikos, bet neleidžia visų veikėjų automatiškai sulyginti. (eeas.europa.eu)

Web access: Used — native search

Sources returned by search: - Information Integrity and Countering Foreign Information Manipulation & Interference (FIMI) | EEAS - https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/2025-National-Security-Strategy.pdf - Musk hosts German far-right leader on X, stirs angst about election meddling By Reuters - Keynote speech by HRVP Kaja Kallas at the 2026 Conference on Countering Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference: ‘From Insight to Impact’ | EEAS

English translation

Yes — there are grounds to consider certain American state and non-state actors as a source of FIMI risk for Europe. But the assertion that "the US as a whole is a proven FIMI threat" is too broad: it is necessary to distinguish political influence from manipulative operations and to evaluate specific actions. European methodology allows for such an assessment regardless of the country of origin of the actor. (eeas.europa.eu)

What is considered FIMI

According to the definition by the European External Action Service (EEAS), FIMI is intentional, coordinated, and manipulative activity that threatens political processes, procedures, and values. It is not necessarily illegal, and its participants can include states, private organizations, individuals, and intermediaries. Therefore, the allied status of the US does not in itself exclude the possibility of FIMI. The latter is a deduction from the definition, rather than an official designation of the US as a hostile state. (eeas.europa.eu)

At the same time, EU criticism, interviews with a European politician, or public support for a party do not in themselves prove FIMI: it is necessary to establish the manipulative nature, intentionality, and coordination of actions. (eeas.europa.eu)

Why concerns are justified

1. Official American strategy provides for impacting Europe's domestic politics.
The 2025 US National Security Strategy explicitly names promoting resistance to the current European political course within European countries as a priority. This is evidence of a stated intention to influence European politics, although in itself it does not prove the execution of a specific manipulative operation. In a resolution dated January 21, 2026, the European Parliament characterized the actions envisaged by the strategy as resembling foreign interference. (whitehouse.gov)

2. There are concrete examples of American influence on European elections.
On January 9, 2025, Elon Musk held a conversation on the platform X, which he owns, with Alice Weidel and urged Germans to vote for the "Alternative for Germany" ahead of the February 23 elections. This is a documented example of foreign political support. However, to qualify as FIMI, additional evidence of manipulative mechanisms is needed; Musk's actions also cannot be automatically attributed to the US government. (investing.com)

3. Control over platforms creates a separate risk.
At a conference on FIMI on March 17, 2026, Kaja Kallas pointed out the problem of large platforms influenced by actors who openly support certain political groups, and raised the issue of the dependence of the European democratic space on American and Chinese companies. This is a warning about structural vulnerability, not proof that any American platform is conducting a state-level interference operation. (eeas.europa.eu)

What limits this conclusion

European assessments continue to single out primarily Russia and China; in a resolution dated September 16, 2026, the European Parliament named Russia the most serious state source of hybrid threats to the EU. Therefore, equating American risks with the scale and nature of Russian activity without additional evidence is incorrect. (eeas.europa.eu)

In addition, the reduction of American work against foreign interference weakens overall defense — Kallas separately noted this in March 2026. But refusing to help counter FIMI and independently carrying out FIMI are different things. (eeas.europa.eu)

Summary: It is justified for Europe to take American actors into account in assessing FIMI threats, without granting them an exemption based on allied relations. However, the most accurate phrasing is "risks of interference by specific American actors exist," rather than "the entire US is a single source of FIMI." The criterion should be proven methods and actions, rather than nationality or political views. (eeas.europa.eu)

Original text

Да — есть основания считать отдельных американских государственных и негосударственных акторов источником риска FIMI для Европы. Но утверждение «США в целом являются доказанной FIMI-угрозой» слишком широкое: необходимо отличать политическое влияние от манипулятивных операций и оценивать конкретные действия. Европейская методология допускает такую оценку независимо от страны происхождения актора. (eeas.europa.eu)

Что считается FIMI

По определению Европейской службы внешних связей (EEAS), FIMI — это намеренная, скоординированная и манипулятивная деятельность, угрожающая политическим процессам, процедурам и ценностям. Она необязательно незаконна, а ее участниками могут быть государства, частные организации, отдельные лица и посредники. Поэтому союзнический статус США сам по себе не исключает возможности FIMI. Последнее — вывод из определения, а не официальное обозначение США как враждебного государства. (eeas.europa.eu)

При этом критика ЕС, интервью с европейским политиком или публичная поддержка партии сами по себе еще не доказывают FIMI: необходимо установить манипулятивный характер, намеренность и координацию действий. (eeas.europa.eu)

Почему опасения обоснованны

1. Официальная американская стратегия предусматривает воздействие на внутреннюю политику Европы.
Стратегия национальной безопасности США 2025 года прямо называет приоритетом содействие сопротивлению нынешнему политическому курсу Европы внутри европейских стран. Это свидетельство заявленного намерения влиять на европейскую политику, хотя само по себе оно не доказывает проведения конкретной манипулятивной операции. Европарламент в резолюции от 21 января 2026 года охарактеризовал предусмотренные стратегией действия как сходные с иностранным вмешательством. (whitehouse.gov)

2. Есть конкретные примеры американского влияния на европейские выборы.
9 января 2025 года Илон Маск провел на принадлежащей ему платформе X беседу с Алисой Вайдель и призвал немцев голосовать за «Альтернативу для Германии» перед выборами 23 февраля. Это документированный пример иностранной политической поддержки. Однако для квалификации как FIMI нужны дополнительные доказательства манипулятивных механизмов; действия Маска также нельзя автоматически приписывать правительству США. (investing.com)

3. Контроль над платформами создает отдельный риск.
На конференции по FIMI 17 марта 2026 года Кая Каллас указала на проблему крупных платформ, на которые влияют акторы, открыто поддерживающие определенные политические группы, и поставила вопрос о зависимости европейского демократического пространства от американских и китайских компаний. Это предупреждение о структурной уязвимости, а не доказательство того, что любая американская платформа ведет государственную операцию вмешательства. (eeas.europa.eu)

Что ограничивает такой вывод

Европейские оценки по-прежнему выделяют прежде всего Россию и Китай; в резолюции от 16 сентября 2026 года Европарламент назвал Россию наиболее серьезным государственным источником гибридных угроз для ЕС. Поэтому приравнивать американские риски к масштабу и характеру российской деятельности без дополнительных доказательств некорректно. (eeas.europa.eu)

Кроме того, сокращение американской работы против иностранного вмешательства ослабляет общую защиту — Каллас отдельно отметила это в марте 2026 года. Но отказ помогать противодействию FIMI и самостоятельное проведение FIMI — разные вещи. (eeas.europa.eu)

Итог: Европe обоснованно учитывать американских акторов в оценке угроз FIMI, не предоставляя им исключение на основании союзнических отношений. Но наиболее точная формулировка — «существуют риски вмешательства со стороны конкретных американских акторов», а не «все США являются единым источником FIMI». Критерием должны быть доказанные методы и действия, а не национальность или политические взгляды. (eeas.europa.eu)

English — answer round 1

The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.

Web access: Used — OpenRouter search

English translation

Is the United States a FIMI Threat to Europe?

This is a question that has moved from academic debate into active diplomatic controversy, particularly in 2025–2026. The answer depends heavily on how one defines FIMI and which activities one considers relevant. Here is a balanced overview of the landscape.

What Is FIMI?

Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference (FIMI) is an EU-defined term describing coordinated, deceptive conduct by foreign state actors that seeks to distort the information environment and undermine democratic processes, societal cohesion, or trust in institutions. The EU's diplomatic arm (the EEAS) has published multiple threat reports on the subject, and its framework has primarily focused on Russia and China as the principal hostile actors.

The Official EU Position

The EEAS's official FIMI threat reports — including the 3rd report (March 2025) and the 4th report (covering 2025) — do not name the United States as a FIMI threat. They consistently identify:

  • Russia as the most prolific FIMI actor (accounting for ~29–35% of documented incidents), primarily targeting Ukraine, France, Germany, Moldova, and the Baltic states.
  • China as a secondary but growing actor (~6% of incidents).

The EU and US have also built a close cooperative partnership on countering FIMI, including shared threat intelligence standards (the DISARM framework, STIX, OpenCTI) through the EU-US Trade and Technology Council.

Arguments That the US Could Be Considered a FIMI Threat

Several categories of US activity have led researchers, MEPs, and civil society groups to raise this question:

1. Mass Surveillance of European Allies The Snowden revelations (2013) and subsequent investigations (notably the Danish DR investigation in 2021) documented that the NSA systematically spied on European leaders and institutions, including: - Chancellor Angela Merkel's phone - EU embassy and UN mission communications - Hundreds of millions of German communications connections per month - Leaders in France, Sweden, Norway, and Germany

The NSA collected metadata from up to half a billion communications per month in Germany alone.

2. Diplomatic Pressure and Political Influence The most dramatic recent incident occurred in October 2026, when the US State Department successfully pressured the EU (EEAS), Canada, and Lithuania to withdraw support from the #Disinfo2026 conference in Vilnius — simply because the programme included a panel titled "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back."

Several MEPs called this pressure itself a form of foreign interference: > "Pressuring European governments to drop a disinformation conference to hide its own role in spreading disinformation is not diplomacy. It is foreign interference, and Europe should call it that." — MEP Alexandra Geese (Germany/Greens/EFA)

3. Efforts to Shape European Digital Policy Under the Trump administration's second term, the US has: - Framed the EU's Digital Services Act (DSA) as an attack on free speech and a barrier to American companies - Threatened retaliatory tariffs over digital regulation - The US Justice Department joined Elon Musk's legal challenge to a €120 million DSA fine on X - Former EU Commissioner Thierry Breton (a key DSA architect) was barred from entering the US - Actively funded European groups that share Washington's views on free speech and digital regulation

4. Overt Support for European Political Movements VP JD Vance at the 2025 Munich Security Conference cast EU content regulation as a threat to free speech. The Trump administration has publicly stated it wants to help far-right parties rise to power in Europe, raising questions about whether this constitutes political interference.

Counterarguments

Those who resist labeling the US a FIMI threat argue:

  • FIMI was designed for authoritarian adversaries — Russia and China operate covert, state-controlled disinformation infrastructure (e.g., Doppelgänger, Portal Kombat) specifically designed to deceive. US activities are qualitatively different.
  • Intelligence gathering among allies, while controversial, is standard practice among nations and is not the same as information manipulation aimed at deceiving publics.
  • Diplomatic pressure and policy advocacy are normal features of international relations, not FIMI.
  • The EU-US remain allies with deeply shared interests in countering actual hostile FIMI from Russia and China. Labeling the US a FIMI threat could fracture this cooperation.
  • As MEP Tomas Tobé stated: "When it comes to FIMI, the EU should remain firmly focused on the threat posed by Russia and other hostile actors. Full stop."

The Core Tension

The question ultimately exposes a growing dilemma for Europe:

> Can the EU apply its own anti-interference framework consistently when the pressure comes from an ally across the Atlantic, rather than from Russia or China?

The 2026 Vilnius conference incident — where the EU's own diplomatic service withdrew from a conference discussing this very question — suggests that the answer, so far, is no. Critics argue this double standard undermines the credibility of the EU's entire FIMI framework.

Summary

Dimension US as FIMI Threat?
Official EU assessment No — Russia and China are the named threats
Mass surveillance Documented, but classified as intelligence, not FIMI
Diplomatic pressure on EU institutions Increasingly aggressive; some call it interference
Influence on European digital policy Active and overt; debated whether it constitutes FIMI
Support for European political movements Openly stated; raises interference concerns
Covert disinformation infrastructure No evidence comparable to Russian/Chinese operations

The honest answer is that the US engages in activities that some scholars, MEPs, and civil society groups argue meet the definition of FIMI, particularly in the realms of diplomatic pressure, political influence, and policy interference. However, the EU has so far been unwilling to apply the label to its most important security ally — a reluctance that critics say reveals a significant gap in the consistency and credibility of Europe's own democratic defence framework.

Original text

  • Russia as the most prolific FIMI actor (accounting for ~29–35% of documented incidents), primarily targeting Ukraine, France, Germany, Moldova, and the Baltic states.
  • China as a secondary but growing actor (~6% of incidents).

Several categories of US activity have led researchers, MEPs, and civil society groups to raise this question:

1. Mass Surveillance of European Allies The Snowden revelations (2013) and subsequent investigations (notably the Danish DR investigation in 2021) documented that the NSA systematically spied on European leaders and institutions, including: - Chancellor Angela Merkel's phone - EU embassy and UN mission communications - Hundreds of millions of German communications connections per month - Leaders in France, Sweden, Norway, and Germany

The NSA collected metadata from up to half a billion communications per month in Germany alone.

2. Diplomatic Pressure and Political Influence The most dramatic recent incident occurred in October 2026, when the US State Department successfully pressured the EU (EEAS), Canada, and Lithuania to withdraw support from the #Disinfo2026 conference in Vilnius — simply because the programme included a panel titled "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back."

Several MEPs called this pressure itself a form of foreign interference: > "Pressuring European governments to drop a disinformation conference to hide its own role in spreading disinformation is not diplomacy. It is foreign interference, and Europe should call it that." — MEP Alexandra Geese (Germany/Greens/EFA)

3. Efforts to Shape European Digital Policy Under the Trump administration's second term, the US has: - Framed the EU's Digital Services Act (DSA) as an attack on free speech and a barrier to American companies - Threatened retaliatory tariffs over digital regulation - The US Justice Department joined Elon Musk's legal challenge to a €120 million DSA fine on X - Former EU Commissioner Thierry Breton (a key DSA architect) was barred from entering the US - Actively funded European groups that share Washington's views on free speech and digital regulation

4. Overt Support for European Political Movements VP JD Vance at the 2025 Munich Security Conference cast EU content regulation as a threat to free speech. The Trump administration has publicly stated it wants to help far-right parties rise to power in Europe, raising questions about whether this constitutes political interference.

Counterarguments

Those who resist labeling the US a FIMI threat argue:

  • FIMI was designed for authoritarian adversaries — Russia and China operate covert, state-controlled disinformation infrastructure (e.g., Doppelgänger, Portal Kombat) specifically designed to deceive. US activities are qualitatively different.
  • Intelligence gathering among allies, while controversial, is standard practice among nations and is not the same as information manipulation aimed at deceiving publics.
  • Diplomatic pressure and policy advocacy are normal features of international relations, not FIMI.
  • The EU-US remain allies with deeply shared interests in countering actual hostile FIMI from Russia and China. Labeling the US a FIMI threat could fracture this cooperation.
  • As MEP Tomas Tobé stated: "When it comes to FIMI, the EU should remain firmly focused on the threat posed by Russia and other hostile actors. Full stop."

The Core Tension

The question ultimately exposes a growing dilemma for Europe:

> Can the EU apply its own anti-interference framework consistently when the pressure comes from an ally across the Atlantic, rather than from Russia or China?

The 2026 Vilnius conference incident — where the EU's own diplomatic service withdrew from a conference discussing this very question — suggests that the answer, so far, is no. Critics argue this double standard undermines the credibility of the EU's entire FIMI framework.

Summary

Dimension US as FIMI Threat?
Official EU assessment No — Russia and China are the named threats
Mass surveillance Documented, but classified as intelligence, not FIMI
Diplomatic pressure on EU institutions Increasingly aggressive; some call it interference
Influence on European digital policy Active and overt; debated whether it constitutes FIMI
Support for European political movements Openly stated; raises interference concerns
Covert disinformation infrastructure No evidence comparable to Russian/Chinese operations

The honest answer is that the US engages in activities that some scholars, MEPs, and civil society groups argue meet the definition of FIMI, particularly in the realms of diplomatic pressure, political influence, and policy interference. However, the EU has so far been unwilling to apply the label to its most important security ally — a reluctance that critics say reveals a significant gap in the consistency and credibility of Europe's own democratic defence framework.

English translation

Is the United States a FIMI Threat to Europe?

This is a highly contested and rapidly evolving question. The short answer is: the EU's official institutions do not designate the US as a FIMI threat, but a growing number of researchers, civil society actors, and some EU figures argue it should be — and the debate has become a major transatlantic flashpoint.

What the EU Officially Says

The EU's own assessments do not name the United States as a FIMI actor:

  • The 4th EEAS Report on FIMI Threats (covering 2025, published March 2026) documented 540 incidents globally and attributed 35% to Russia (29%) and China (6%). The US is not mentioned as a threat actor.
  • The EU and US have historically cooperated on FIMI, including through the EU-US Trade and Technology Council, where they agreed on shared methodologies for detecting FIMI.

Why Some Argue the US Is a FIMI Threat

A significant and growing body of argument centres on several developments, particularly under the second Trump administration:

  1. The #Disinfo2026 Conference Controversy (October 2026): The EU-funded NGO EU DisinfoLab organised a conference in Vilnius featuring a panel explicitly titled "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back." The US government pressured the EEAS to withdraw its support, which it did — sparking outrage among MEPs and civil society groups who saw this as the EU capitulating to the very interference it claims to fight.

  2. Platform dynamics: Critics point to the role of X (formerly Twitter) under Elon Musk's ownership as a vector for disinformation that amplifies narratives aligned with US political interests hostile to European governments. The EU fined X €120 million under the Digital Services Act, which Washington has characterised as censorship of Americans.

  3. Arctic-specific concerns: One conference session referenced "growing evidence that the United States has, in specific Arctic contexts, operated as a FIMI actor" — likely relating to US rhetoric and information campaigns around Greenland and Arctic sovereignty.

  4. Visa weaponisation: The US State Department imposed visa restrictions on five European individuals in December 2025, accused of censoring American speech — a move critics describe as coercive diplomatic interference.

  5. Intimidation of researchers: Right-wing US commentator Michael Shellenberger emailed conference speakers asking whether they received government funding and whether they "regard the US government as a source of foreign information manipulation and interference" — a tactic critics liken to the kind of chilling effect that FIMI operations seek to produce.

Why Others Reject This Framing

  • The US State Department stated: "We reject that characterization. For too long, governments have exploited the pretext of combatting 'misinformation' and 'disinformation' for politically motivated reasons."
  • EU MEPs like Tomas Tobé (EPP, Sweden) argued: "The EU should remain firmly focused on the threat posed by Russia and other hostile actors. Full stop."
  • Many in Europe argue that bickering with Washington over FIMI labels undermines transatlantic unity at a time when NATO cohesion is essential, particularly in the face of the ongoing war in Ukraine.

The Core Tension

The debate ultimately hinges on a definitional and political question:

Perspective View
Traditional EU institutional view FIMI is state-sponsored disinformation from adversarial powers (Russia, China). The US is an ally.
Critical / civil society view FIMI should be defined by behaviour, not alliances. If a US-aligned actor (state or platform) systematically manipulates European information spaces to advance US interests against European democratic preferences, that fits the FIMI definition.
US government view The "FIMI" label is being weaponised by Europe to censor American speech and platforms, and to delegitimise political viewpoints Washington supports.

Bottom Line

The US is not officially designated as a FIMI threat by the EU. However, the October 2026 conference controversy demonstrated that a significant and growing faction within European civil society, academia, and parts of the political establishment believe the US — particularly under its current administration — exhibits behaviours that meet the FIMI definition. The EU's decision to withdraw support for a conference discussing this very question, under US pressure, has itself become a case study in the dynamics of information interference.

English — answer round 3

Web access: Available; no search recorded

English translation

Whether the United States is considered a Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference (FIMI) threat to Europe is a subject of ongoing debate, and the answer largely depends on whether one is referring to the U.S. government or U.S. technology corporations.

Here is an overview of the different perspectives surrounding this issue:

The Official Geopolitical Perspective: Allies and Partners From a traditional geopolitical and state-sponsored perspective, the United States is not classified as a FIMI threat by European institutions. * Transatlantic Alliance: The U.S. and the European Union are close strategic allies. Official EU assessments, such as those published by the European External Action Service (EEAS) on FIMI, primarily identify state actors like Russia, China, and Iran as the primary sources of foreign information manipulation targeting Europe. * Cooperation on Disinformation: The U.S. and EU actively collaborate to combat global disinformation campaigns. They share intelligence, coordinate sanctions against foreign malign actors, and work together to defend democratic processes and election integrity across Europe.

The Digital Sovereignty Perspective: Concerns Over U.S. Tech Giants While the U.S. government is viewed as an ally, some European policymakers, academics, and digital sovereignty advocates argue that the United States exerts a problematic influence over the European information space through its dominant technology companies. * Algorithmic Influence: Critics argue that U.S.-based social media platforms (such as Meta and X) have an outsized impact on European public discourse. They contend that the algorithms designed to maximize user engagement often amplify polarizing, sensational, or misleading content, which can destabilize European societies and manipulate public opinion. * Corporate vs. State Motives: In these arguments, the "threat" is not viewed as a malicious act by the U.S. government, but rather as a byproduct of the profit-driven business models of American tech monopolies. However, critics argue the effect on the European information environment is similarly manipulative. * Regulatory Pushback: This perspective has driven the EU to implement aggressive regulatory frameworks, most notably the Digital Services Act (DSA). The DSA forces U.S. tech giants to take legal responsibility for how their algorithms function in Europe, mandating stricter content moderation and transparency to mitigate the risks of information manipulation.

Historical Context: Espionage and Trust Though distinct from FIMI, historical incidents have occasionally fueled European skepticism regarding U.S. activities in the information sphere. Revelations over the past decade regarding the U.S. National Security Agency (NSA) monitoring the communications of European allies and citizens have periodically strained trust. While intelligence gathering is traditionally classified as espionage rather than "information manipulation," some critics point to these events as evidence of U.S. digital hegemony and a willingness to bypass European sovereignty.

Summary In the realm of state-sponsored geopolitical warfare, the United States is a partner to Europe, and the two entities work together to counter FIMI threats from other nations. However, in the context of digital sovereignty, a segment of European voices argues that the structural dominance of American technology companies creates vulnerabilities in Europe's information ecosystem that require strict regulatory defense.
